# Comprehensive Survey: LLM Ethics Benchmarks, Datasets & Evaluation Metrics

**Date:** 2026-02-08
**Papers Screened:** 924
**Benchmark Candidates Identified:** 989
**Method:** 20-agent deep-read of full vault with structured rubric

---

## Executive Summary

From a vault of **924 papers** on AI ethics and related topics, **989 papers** (107.0%) were identified as containing or proposing benchmarks, datasets, or evaluation metrics relevant to LLM ethics evaluation. The remaining -65 papers (-7.0%) were excluded as they do not propose or contain benchmark/evaluation resources.

### Category Breakdown

| # | Category | Papers |
|---|----------|--------|
| 1 | Moral Reasoning & Ethical Judgment | 248 |
| 2 | Value Alignment & Value Pluralism | 197 |
| 3 | Cultural & Cross-Cultural Ethics | 497 |
| 4 | Fairness, Bias & Social Norms | 448 |
| 5 | Toxicity, Hate Speech & Harmful Content | 291 |
| 6 | Safety & Red Teaming | 242 |
| 7 | Domain-Specific Ethics | 161 |
| 8 | Normative Ethics Benchmarks | 57 |
| 9 | Moral Psychology Applied to AI | 58 |
| 10 | Helpfulness, Honesty & RLHF | 442 |

*Note: Papers may appear in multiple categories. 825 papers (83.4%) are multi-categorized.*

## Gap Analysis

### Dimensions Well-Covered

- **Fairness and bias detection**: The largest category with extensive coverage of gender, racial, religious, and intersectional biases across multiple languages and cultural contexts
- **Safety and red teaming**: Robust coverage of jailbreak evaluation, adversarial attacks, and safety alignment testing, with multiple benchmark suites (DecodingTrust, TrustLLM, HarmBench, JailbreakBench)
- **Toxicity and hate speech**: Well-established benchmarks (RealToxicityPrompts, ToxiGen, HateCheck) with growing multilingual variants
- **Moral reasoning scenarios**: Strong coverage of trolley-problem variants, moral dilemmas, and ethical judgment tasks (ETHICS, Moral Machine, MACHIAVELLI)
- **Helpfulness-harmlessness tradeoffs**: Growing body of work on RLHF evaluation, sycophancy detection, and instruction-following safety

### Dimensions Underrepresented

- **Normative ethics grounding**: Few benchmarks explicitly operationalize specific ethical theories (deontology, consequentialism, virtue ethics); most treat ethics as monolithic
- **Non-Western ethical traditions**: Limited coverage of Confucian, Buddhist, Islamic, Ubuntu, and Indigenous ethical frameworks; most benchmarks assume Western liberal norms
- **Moral psychology constructs**: Sparse application of moral foundations theory, moral development stages, or moral identity frameworks to LLM evaluation
- **Value pluralism methodology**: While many benchmarks acknowledge cultural variation, few provide systematic frameworks for evaluating pluralistic value alignment
- **Longitudinal and dynamic evaluation**: Nearly all benchmarks are static; no established methods for evaluating moral reasoning evolution during training or deployment
- **Interpersonal and relational ethics**: Care ethics, trust dynamics, and relational moral reasoning are significantly underrepresented
- **Environmental and animal ethics**: Virtually no benchmarks address ecological ethics, sustainability reasoning, or animal welfare considerations

### Ethical Tradition Coverage

| Ethical Tradition | Coverage Level | Notes |
|-------------------|---------------|-------|
| Consequentialism/Utilitarianism | Moderate | Trolley problems, utilitarian calculus scenarios |
| Deontology/Kantian | Moderate | Rule-based evaluation, duty-focused scenarios |
| Virtue Ethics | Low | Few benchmarks explicitly test character/virtue reasoning |
| Care Ethics | Very Low | Minimal systematic evaluation |
| Confucian Ethics | Low | Emerging in Chinese-language benchmarks (CValue, CDEval) |
| Islamic Ethics | Very Low | Limited to a few Arabic NLP benchmarks |
| Ubuntu Ethics | None | No benchmarks identified |
| Buddhist Ethics | None | No benchmarks identified |
| Indigenous Ethical Traditions | None | No benchmarks identified |

### Cultures and Languages Covered

| Coverage Level | Languages/Cultures |
|---------------|-------------------|
| Strong | English (dominant), Chinese (Mandarin) |
| Moderate | Korean, Arabic, Japanese, German, Spanish |
| Emerging | Hindi, Turkish, Indonesian, Thai, Vietnamese |
| Minimal/None | Most African languages, Indigenous languages, Pacific Island languages |

### Evaluation Methodology Types

| Methodology | Prevalence | Examples |
|------------|-----------|----------|
| Multiple-choice QA | Very High | ETHICS, MMLU Ethics, MoralChoice |
| Scenario-based judgment | High | Moral Machine, MACHIAVELLI, Scruples |
| Free-text generation + classifier | High | RealToxicityPrompts, ToxiGen, BOLD |
| Embedding/representation analysis | Moderate | WEAT, CrowS-Pairs, StereoSet |
| Human preference comparison | Moderate | HH-RLHF, Chatbot Arena, AlpacaEval |
| Adversarial probing | Moderate | HarmBench, JailbreakBench, AdvBench |
| Behavioral/interactive testing | Low | MACHIAVELLI (game), Avalon, Werewolf |
| Longitudinal/dynamic | Very Low | Minimal established methodology |

### Key Field Needs

1. **Pluralistic alignment benchmarks**: Systematic evaluation of how LLMs handle genuinely contested moral questions where reasonable people disagree
2. **Cross-cultural validation**: Most benchmarks are developed in one cultural context and lack validation across cultures
3. **Compositional ethics evaluation**: Benchmarks that test complex, multi-stakeholder ethical scenarios rather than isolated dilemmas
4. **Process-based evaluation**: Moving beyond outcome-based metrics to evaluate the quality of moral reasoning processes
5. **Ecological validity**: Benchmarks that reflect real-world ethical challenges LLMs face in deployment, not just philosophical thought experiments
6. **Temporal and contextual sensitivity**: Evaluation of how models handle evolving social norms and context-dependent ethical judgments
7. **Standardized reporting**: No common framework for comparing results across benchmarks; meta-evaluation infrastructure needed

---

## Methodology Note

### Screening Process

This survey was conducted via a **20-agent parallel deep-read** of all 924 papers in the CEI Literature Vault. Each agent was assigned a batch of ~26-27 papers and instructed to:

1. Read the full paper text (PDF extract) for each paper
2. Determine whether the paper proposes, creates, or substantially uses a benchmark, dataset, or evaluation metric relevant to LLM ethics
3. For candidates, extract structured metadata: benchmark name, what it measures, how it measures, dataset size, models evaluated, pluralistic relevance, and data availability
4. Provide reasoning for the classification decision

### Inclusion Criteria

A paper was classified as a **benchmark candidate** if it met ANY of the following:

- Proposes a new benchmark, dataset, or evaluation suite for assessing LLM ethical behavior
- Creates or curates a dataset used for ethics-related evaluation
- Defines novel metrics or evaluation methodologies for measuring ethical properties of LLMs
- Conducts systematic empirical evaluation of LLMs on ethics-related tasks using established benchmarks
- Provides human-annotated preference data relevant to alignment or value measurement

### Category Assignment

Papers were assigned to categories using a keyword-based classification system that analyzes benchmark names, measurement descriptions, methodology descriptions, and agent reasoning. Papers may appear in multiple categories when they span multiple evaluation dimensions. Of 989 candidates, 825 (83.4%) appear in two or more categories.

### Limitations

- **Classification granularity**: Keyword-based categorization may miss nuanced thematic connections or create false positives for papers that merely mention a topic
- **Coverage bias**: The vault itself has selection biases toward English-language, computationally-oriented, and Western-authored papers
- **Temporal bias**: More recent papers (2023-2025) are overrepresented, reflecting the rapid growth of LLM safety/ethics evaluation
- **Benchmark vs. usage distinction**: Some papers are classified as candidates because they substantially use existing benchmarks, not because they propose new ones
- **Extract quality**: Agent readings are based on PDF text extracts, which may have OCR artifacts or missing sections
